one-level rates = the level median of each process with one level.

reading: rule 3 pools the 2 'read' and the 2 'asmread' processes at 8 MiB of one CPU as 'the 4 load-only processes'; the within-level spread is the largest half range over both levels of those processes.

8. H2: the original program at 8 MiB (bw_old = old read, bw_new = new read)

A new-read repetition is low at or below 69.5 GB/s and high at or above 70.5 GB/s.
gain = 100 x (new / old - 1) of the process medians of a pair of neighbouring processes.
reading: 'the low processes' of the H2 rule are the pairs whose new-read process has a median repetition rate at or below 69.5 GB/s; the 'within 1.5 percentage points of 0.94 %' condition must hold in both sessions.

9. YL: window length (rule 1 of round 3, hypothesis H7)

first fall, first up, switches = switch windows (record indices). u = up, f = fall.
reading: rule 1 is decided on the 100 ms and 1000 ms runs, because at 300 ms the index prediction and the time prediction give the same windows; a median matches the index prediction if it is within 1 window of 16 or 42 (first fall) or of 21 or 44 (first up), and the time prediction if it is within 1 window of the window that holds 4.93 or 12.76 s (fall) or 6.3 or 13.2 s (up); kernels 'read' and 'asmread' are pooled; a median that does not exist (no process shows that switch) is left out.
reading: one-window runs: the 10 ms bins are joined in groups of 10 (0.1 s), levels9 segments these rates, and a switch counts if its est. time is more than 0.3 s after the window start.

10. YP: record pad (rule 2 of round 3, hypothesis H7)

The statistic that rule 2 fixed ('the first fall after window 30') is replaced by the series of all up
and fall switch windows, because that fall is absent in some processes. It is still printed where it
exists.
shift = (nearest pad 0 switch window of the same CPU and session) minus (switch window of the padded
run). A positive shift means the padded run switches earlier. Prediction: pad / 80 windows.
reading: each up of a padded run is matched to the nearest pad 0 up, so a shift is known only modulo the spacing of the pad 0 ups (about 23 windows); the prediction pad / 80 is therefore also reduced modulo that spacing before the 'within 1 window' comparison.
reading: the falls 'at windows 70 and 95 of pad 0' are the recurring pad 0 falls: the falls of the first pad 0 process that every other pad 0 process of the CPU and session also has within 1 window (the switch window of a fall is the window in which the rate falls, 69 and 95 in session 1); for each of them the nearest fall of each padded process gives one shift value, and the comparison is modulo the spacing of these falls; the falls are descriptive.
reading: rule 2 is decided on the up series: 'supported' if at least 4 of the 5 pads match on both CPUs, 'rejected' if the median shift is 0 (at most 0.5) for every pad; the --hot-copy prediction (median switch count at most 1 over the hot-copy processes of a session) is reported beside the verdict.

11. YC and YF: the prefetch family (rule 3 of round 3, hypothesis H1b)

reading: in rule 3 the rate of a prefetch kernel is its YF cell p95 at 8 MiB and the 'asmread high level' is the asmread YF cell p95 at 8 MiB of the same CPU and session; the miss share uses the YC medians over all counted windows of a process.
reading: repeatability rule for 'prefetch kernel minus asmread': the unit value is the process median over all windows, pairs are the processes with the same rep_set, and the A/A value of a process is median(repeat 1, repeat 3) minus median(repeat 2, repeat 4) of the per-trial median rates.

14. ZM: kick map (rule 3 of round 4, descriptive, no decision rule)

position in the pad 0 layout). Level before = level of the last window on a level before the boundary.

====================================================================================================
READINGS (all 'reading:' choices of this output)
  - a fall or an up is 'recurring' when at least 3 processes of the session show it inside one cluster (pooled switch times cut at gaps above 0.15 s); CPUs and the two load-only kernels are pooled.
  - the S4 first-up time of rule 5 is the fixed value 6.4 s that the rule names; the median is taken over the processes of both CPUs of a session that show an up switch.
  - rule 3 pools the 2 'read' and the 2 'asmread' processes at 8 MiB of one CPU as 'the 4 load-only processes'; the within-level spread is the largest half range over both levels of those processes.
  - the S4 levels of a CPU are the medians over the load-only S4 processes with two levels, over all S4 sessions that exist; step XW (sampler, not counted) is checked the same way.
  - in rule 6 the 'asmread 8 MiB high level' is the asmread cell p95 at 8 MiB and the 'low level' is its cell p5, both from XN of the same CPU and session.
  - in rule 2 of round 4, S8 is the range from the cell p5 to the cell p95 of asmread at 8 MiB of the same CPU and session, from YF if it exists, else from ZD without evict; the limit for a kernel between 66 and 76 GB/s is applied to its 8 MiB p95.
  - 'the low processes' of the H2 rule are the pairs whose new-read process has a median repetition rate at or below 69.5 GB/s; the 'within 1.5 percentage points of 0.94 %' condition must hold in both sessions.
  - rule 1 is decided on the 100 ms and 1000 ms runs, because at 300 ms the index prediction and the time prediction give the same windows; a median matches the index prediction if it is within 1 window of 16 or 42 (first fall) or of 21 or 44 (first up), and the time prediction if it is within 1 window of the window that holds 4.93 or 12.76 s (fall) or 6.3 or 13.2 s (up); kernels 'read' and 'asmread' are pooled; a median that does not exist (no process shows that switch) is left out.
  - one-window runs: the 10 ms bins are joined in groups of 10 (0.1 s), levels9 segments these rates, and a switch counts if its est. time is more than 0.3 s after the window start.
  - each up of a padded run is matched to the nearest pad 0 up, so a shift is known only modulo the spacing of the pad 0 ups (about 23 windows); the prediction pad / 80 is therefore also reduced modulo that spacing before the 'within 1 window' comparison.
  - the falls 'at windows 70 and 95 of pad 0' are the recurring pad 0 falls: the falls of the first pad 0 process that every other pad 0 process of the CPU and session also has within 1 window (the switch window of a fall is the window in which the rate falls, 69 and 95 in session 1); for each of them the nearest fall of each padded process gives one shift value, and the comparison is modulo the spacing of these falls; the falls are descriptive.
  - rule 2 is decided on the up series: 'supported' if at least 4 of the 5 pads match on both CPUs, 'rejected' if the median shift is 0 (at most 0.5) for every pad; the --hot-copy prediction (median switch count at most 1 over the hot-copy processes of a session) is reported beside the verdict.
  - in rule 3 the rate of a prefetch kernel is its YF cell p95 at 8 MiB and the 'asmread high level' is the asmread YF cell p95 at 8 MiB of the same CPU and session; the miss share uses the YC medians over all counted windows of a process.
  - repeatability rule for 'prefetch kernel minus asmread': the unit value is the process median over all windows, pairs are the processes with the same rep_set, and the A/A value of a process is median(repeat 1, repeat 3) minus median(repeat 2, repeat 4) of the per-trial median rates.
  - the check uses, per CPU, the median over the ZC evict processes of asmread at 8 MiB of l2d_cache_wb per 64 B, and it passes only if every CPU is below 0.2; 'supported' needs a rise of at least 3.0 GB/s for both kernels on both CPUs, 'rejected' needs every rise below 1.0 GB/s, anything else is 'partly'; the old read control uses the 8 MiB cell medians.
  - a size is 'outside' if its cycles per 64 B lie outside the closed band; sizes 1 and 8 MiB are printed and not counted; with more than one process in a cell the cell value is the median over the processes.
  - H3a (a kernel timer on the worker's CPU triggers the switch) is 'not supported' in a session if no listed sampler key accompanies the switches by rule 4; rule 4 names a coinciding activity and cannot confirm a cause.
  - supplementary: the reference series U (ups) and the reference series of the falls are the switch windows at which at least 5 of the load-only S4 processes of round 1 show the switch (the sessions that exist are pooled, the window must be the same).
  - supplementary: the prediction for pad P is every reference window minus P / 80, cut to the windows of the run (0 to 99); an event 'matches' within 1 window; a process is 'at the low level before window w' when the last window up to w - 2 that sits on a level is low; a process with one level gets the nearer of the two level medians of the two-level YP processes of its CPU and session.
